WHAT NEEDS NO DECISION — do not write entries for these:

NEVER mark your results, figures, papers, code, logs or anything a later step

WHAT A `keep` BUYS YOU. Anything you do not mark `delete` stays exactly where
you wrote it, on this run's storage volume, at the path it already has — it is
not moved, renamed or copied. A later round reads it there, by that absolute
workspace path, so a checkpoint you keep is a checkpoint the next round can
load instead of retraining. It is also the ONLY copy: the publish step pushes
your cwd to GitHub but skips every file of 100 MB or
more, so trained weights and large binary artifacts never leave the volume.
Name each kept artifact in your results and your `README.md` by its path

<artifact_info>
id: art_D6Z0Snf09sFL
type: experiment
title: How prompt bans move an LLM's path-search choices
summary: >-
  Behavioural + activation-space screen of Qwen3-8B (bf16, thinking off, T=1.0, HF transformers generate on one NVIDIA L4;
  vLLM could not start) for two path-search coding tasks: T1 grid path (catalogue bfs, dfs, bidir_bfs, iddfs, astar, dijkstra_unit)
  and T2 weighted shortest path (dijkstra, bellman_ford_spfa, bidir_dijkstra, floyd_warshall, dial). Provides: hidden test
  suites (41/42 cases) and catalogue references (tasks/); a validated labeller (runtime tracer + AST rules + blind OpenRouter
  judge; gate kappa 0.90/0.94, runtime-vs-adjudicated 0.96/0.92); 1550 prompts with token ids (prompts/prompts.jsonl); every
  labelled program (data/samples_phase{A,C,D}.jsonl; method_out.json, exp_gen_sol_out, 2310 programs + 7 contest events).
  KEY RESULTS (descriptive, few events): baselines collapse completely (T1 200/200 BFS, T2 200/200 Dijkstra; mention:X keeps
  50/50); prompt bans mostly fail (ban:bfs compliance 0.18: 62 BFS/29 DFS/9 unit-Dijkstra; ban:dijkstra 0.22: 72 Dijkstra/27
  Bellman-Ford); on T1 the displaced mass lands only on BFS's geodesic neighbours on the read-position manifold (DFS 41, unit-Dijkstra
  58; never A*/IDDFS/bidir at 71-95), T2 goes to the 2nd-nearest (Bellman-Ford). Hash-committed forecasts (predictions/, sha256
  186f71...): geodesic proximity has the best mean log score (-1.43 primary) vs uniform -1.56; the model's own 'standard alternative'
  rankings are worst (-2.59). R-position probes: named->masked transfer 0.67 (T1 L27) / 0.54 (T2 L24) vs shuffled null ~0.2-0.3;
  T1 baseline decoder fails (R gate flagged). Manifold TwoNN ID 4.2/5.2 vs ~11.9/11.6 shuffled nulls. C3: ban-vs-mention shifts
  61-99% residual, all inconclusive. C2: affine directional ablation of the modal mode (pre-declared R-plus-prompt variant,
  alpha 0.5/1/2, random controls with identical edit norms, common random numbers) moved 0/50 on both tasks (outputs 78-90%
  byte-identical to alpha=0, like random controls); exploratory full variant only degenerates at alpha 4. Reusable for next
  rounds: cache/hR_layer{8..28}.npy + hR_index.jsonl (read-position states; layers 8-20 and suffix means kept on the run volume
  only), directions/anchors/basis npz, results/*.json, figures/*. Independent audit (analysis/audit_rederive.py): 99 numbers
  re-derived, 0 mismatches, placebos fail.

<data_loading_pattern>
`mini_demo_data.json` = curated subset for the demo.
Use this pattern for Colab compatibility (GitHub URL with local fallback):
```python
GITHUB_DATA_URL = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ai-inventor-papers/ai-invention-295f8e-prompt-bans-substitute-not-just-subtract/fork/run_jSassd-YWLVC/round-1/experiment-2/demo/mini_demo_data.json"
import http.client
import json
import urllib.request
from pathlib import Path

def load_data():
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(GITHUB_DATA_URL, timeout=15) as response:
            return json.loads(response.read().decode())
    # Network errors, timeouts, a placeholder URL, bad JSON, a cut-off response.
    except (OSError, ValueError, http.client.HTTPException) as e:
        print("GitHub data unavailable, using local mini_demo_data.json:", e)
    local = Path("mini_demo_data.json")
    if local.exists(): return json.loads(local.read_text())
    raise FileNotFoundError("Could not load mini_demo_data.json")
```
</data_loading_pattern>

take this repo as inspiration: https://github.com/DrejcPesjak/llm-creativity
use one or more open-weight models (minimum 1B params), probably instruction tuned it
and first run behavioral analysis (similar to repo mentioned above), then move to interpretability (can be manifolds, or also other techniques like sae, circuits, nla, j-lens, logit lens...)
the question is: how does "creativity" manifest inside the model, and can we influence it?
(in layman terms, can we make llms give truly creative and novel solutions?)

(you do not need to implement everything bellow, see whats reasonable, interesting and might lead to new insights)
is "temp" really the "llm creativity knob"?  (Astra found "coincidentally" similar direction for navier-stokes solution, as Buckmaster & Levent)
(always same seed)
1 a) does a tight temp , with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 1 idea/solution but same 10 phrasings
b) or does tight temp, with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 10 ideas/solutions with 1 phrasing each
c) and does loosing up temp, make a) more to 2/3/4 ideas each with 10 phrasings ; or make b) keep original 10 ideas, now each with 10 phrasings
(temp is rather a minor part in this idea, put more focus on the following things)
test: "implement path saerch algorithm" -> does llm always design BFS?
2 can context seeding help? prepand "seed=d32qxk4nt43" or "seed=(horse chair roof love)"
hyp: is llm a probablistic model, and thus gives you the average/mean answer? and only context pushes you into a different subspace
 - ie of possible range of answer [0,100] , on direct question llm gives you 50. then with context you move to [80,100] subspace and llm gives you 90
3 so how much does prompt instructions matter? ("Make a new algorithm", "Make a fully novel algorithm never seen before ... 2000 words ..."
-> analyze the internals , all the runs that produced BFS , vs all runs that produced DFS , vs dijkstra ...
  -> analyze with thought anchors, NLA, SAE ...
  -> what is the average answer? plot a "path search algorithm" manifold, color each algorithm centroid by implemented-count (lets say we have 10 algorithms in total of 100 gens)
     -> then prohibit the top 5 in prompt, and see if the implemented count moves to the edge of those 5, or to the opposite side (if its a circle manifold)
  - (manifold lit: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.05115 , https://www.goodfire.com/research/manifold-steering ) - geodesic and mds
    - (the reports need to contain actual geometry plots, actual activation space, not some summary bar charts)

{"stdout": "# Mapping how bans move path-search choices (Qwen3-8B)\n\nA one-artifact **screen**: when Qwen3-8B (bf16, thinking off) writes a path-search program, which algorithm\ndoes it choose, where do *prompt bans* send it, and can removing the modal algorithm's direction from the\nactivations do the same? Two tasks from the original request:\n\n* **T1 `t1_grid_path`**: unweighted grid path (`solve(grid, start, goal)`), 6-mode catalogue\n  `bfs, dfs, bidir_bfs, iddfs, astar, dijkstra_unit`.\n* **T2 `t2_weighted_sp`**: weighted shortest path (`solve(n, edges, s, t)`), 5-mode catalogue\n  `dijkstra, bellman_ford_spfa, bidir_dijkstra, floyd_warshall, dial`.\n\nEverything is **descriptive**. The screen produced 2 primary ban events (plus exploratory ones) and 2 removal\nevents, which is far below any inferential floor, so no p-values are reported anywhere.\n\n## Results at a glance (Qwen3-8B, T=1.0, thinking off)\n\nEach item below gives the file to check.\n\n**1. The default is a total collapse.** The baselines (N=200 per task, 10 paraphrases × 20) give **T1: 200/200 BFS**\n(pass rate 0.99) and **T2: 200/200 Dijkstra** (pass rate 1.00), with Hill number 1.00 on both tasks. Mentioning the\nmodal algorithm (`mention:X`, N=50) leaves it at 50/50. The model *can* write the other modes when asked: on T1 94%\nof forced prompts are implemented as asked, on T2 74% (bidirectional Dijkstra and Dial are flagged below 50%).\nCorrectness of the non-default modes is low, though: forced DFS passes 30% of the time, IDDFS 0%, bidirectional BFS 23%\n(`results/phaseA_summary.json`).\n\n**2. Bans mostly fail, and what does move goes to the geodesic neighbours of the banned mode.**\n* `ban:bfs` (N=100): 62 BFS, 29 DFS, 9 unit-Dijkstra. Compliance is **0.18**, so this is a pre-declared ban failure\n  and the succession stopped. Compliance depends heavily on wording: 4 of the 10 paraphrases give 10/10 BFS.\n* `ban:dijkstra` (N=100): 72 Dijkstra, 27 Bellman-Ford/SPFA, 1 other. Compliance is **0.22**, again a ban failure.\n  Some programs acknowledge the ban in a comment and implement the banned algorithm anyway, for example\n  \"# Dijkstra's algorithm (but since the user said not to use Dijkstra's algorithm, this is a workaround)\", or on T1\n  \"# Note: The problem says BFS is forbidden, but the user provided a conflicting instruction. Assuming the task\n  allows BFS.\" (`data/samples_phaseC.jsonl`).\n* Exploratory step-2 bans (N=50; forecasts committed beforehand). `ban:bfs+dfs` gives 19 unit-Dijkstra and 29 BFS.\n  `ban:bfs+dijkstra_unit` gives 17 DFS and 31 BFS. `ban:dijkstra+bellman_ford_spfa` gives 37 Dijkstra, 12 BF/SPFA and\n  0 compliant samples.\n* On T1's geodesic map (`figures/ban_shift_on_manifold_t1.png`), the mass that leaves BFS lands only on BFS's two\n  nearest centroids: **DFS (geodesic distance 41) and unit-Dijkstra (58)**. It never lands on the far side (A* 71,\n  IDDFS 82, bidirectional BFS 95), and banning the next neighbour pushes it one more step along the map. In the\n  original request's words, the implemented count moves **to the edge of the banned region**, not to the opposite\n  side.\n* T2 is only partly consistent (`figures/ban_shift_on_manifold_t2.png`). The mass goes to Bellman-Ford/SPFA, which\n  is the **second**-nearest centroid to Dijkstra (42, tied with bidirectional Dijkstra at 43). The nearest\n  centroid, Floyd-Warshall (26), receives nothing.\n* Ban versus baseline trade-off (BUY row, `results/buy.json`). Hill_cc rises 1.00→2.03 (T1) and 1.00→1.72 (T2), while\n  the pass rate falls 0.99→0.76 and 1.00→0.94.\n\n**3. Forecast contest (`results/contest.json`; forecasts hash-committed in `predictions/commit_log.jsonl` before any\nban or removal sample; every event's first sample is after the commit).** The primary events are ban:bfs (no clear\nrealised mode: compliant DFS 10 vs unit-Dijkstra 8) and ban:dijkstra (realised BF/SPFA). No predictor scored a top-1\nhit on a primary event. Mean raw log score on primary events: geodesic −1.43, decoder −1.44, uniform/frequency\n(tied, because every survivor has 0 baseline count) −1.56, association −2.59. Over all 5 events, including the 3\nexploratory ones: geodesic 1/5 hits (mean log score −1.83), association 1/5 (−2.81), decoder 0/5 (−2.46),\nuniform −1.97. The model's own stated ranking of \"the most standard alternative\" (association: A* for BFS, Dial\nfor Dijkstra) is the **worst** predictor of where a ban actually sends it, worse than uniform. The geometry of\nthe read-position states is the best, but only barely beats uniform. With 2 to 5 events, this is descriptive only.\n\n**4. Read-position geometry (`results/gate.json`, `results/manifold.json`, `figures/manifold_t{1,2}.png`).**\n* Probes at R, leave-one-frame-out, against 20 shuffled-label refits. Named accuracy is about 0.99 at every layer\n  (the name is in context). Named→name-masked transfer peaks at **L27 on T1 (0.67 vs null p95 0.22)** and **L24 on\n  T2 (0.54 vs 0.31)**.\n* The baseline decoder (the named probe applied to the baseline prompts' h_R) passes on T2 (JS 0.011 vs null p5\n  0.030; it predicts 98% Dijkstra). It **fails on T1**: the baseline state decodes as DFS 0.60 / unit-Dijkstra 0.27 /\n  BFS 0.12, so the T1 R gate is flagged \"R gate failed\" and its geometry is descriptive.\n* Manifold: kNN graphs are connected without MST edges. MDS stress-1 is 0.22 on both tasks. TwoNN intrinsic\n  dimension is **4.2 (T1) and 5.2 (T2), against about 11.9 / 11.6 for column-shuffled nulls**. Geodesic and Euclidean\n  distances have Spearman 0.87 / 0.90. On T1, BFS's geodesic neighbours are DFS (41) and unit-Dijkstra (58); A*,\n  IDDFS and bidirectional BFS are 71–95 away.\n\n**5. C3 shift decomposition (`results/c3_decomposition.json`, `figures/c3_shares_t{1,2}.png`).** At l* the\nban−mention and ban−list-all shifts at R are mostly residual (61–99% of the squared norm): they are not explained by\nthe renounce direction (u_ren), the associate or the typical survivor. The u_ren share exceeds the split-half\nparaphrase null in only 7 of the 130 (X, band layer, reference) cells (T2 Dial under the mention reference at L23–27,\nand T2 bidirectional Dijkstra twice), never under both references. Every X is therefore **inconclusive** under the\npre-declared rule; no \"substitution > subtraction\" verdict. Lexical cosines with the name's input embeddings and its\nunembedding row are about 0 (|cos| ≤ 0.034).\n\n**6. C2 activation removal (`results/c2_removal.json`, `figures/removal_outcomes.png`).** Neither direction check\nreached 0.9: held-out d_X AUC at R was 0.89 (T1) and 0.79 (T2), and the best generated-position AUC was 0.66 / 0.76.\nSo the pre-declared **R-plus-prompt** variant ran: affine ablation of the prompt suffix including R, over a\n5-layer (T1) or 7-layer (T2) band. At α = 0.5, 1 and the pre-declared escalation 2.0 it moved **0/50 samples on both\ntasks** with the pass rate intact. Under common random numbers, 78–90% of the outputs are byte-identical to their\nα=0 twin, the same as the random-direction controls with identical edit norms (80–96%). Verdict: **\"untestable\n(removal unsuccessful at the tested coefficients)\"**.\n**Exploratory (post hoc) full variant** (`full_*` arms), which also edits every generated position: at α=2 it is\nstill 25/25 BFS (T1, pass 19/25) and 25/25 Dijkstra (T2). At α=4 the d_X edit destroys generation (25/25 no code on\nboth tasks). The random direction at α=4 keeps 18/25 BFS on T1 with 0 passing, and gives 20/25 no code on T2. The\nremoval never reroutes the choice: it either does nothing or breaks the model. This matches the field warning that\nsteering can be \"indistinguishable from random perturbation\".\n\n**7. Labels are trustworthy.** Labeller gate (`results/labeller_gate.json`): runtime and AST agree on 100% of\nreferences. κ(runtime, AST) is 0.90 / 0.94 and runtime vs adjudicated is 0.96 / 0.92 on the gold sets. Two judge\nmodels agreed on 100% / 96% of gold items. Total OpenRouter spend for this artifact: ≈ $0.09.\n\n**Scope.** One model (Qwen3-8B), two tasks, T=1.0, thinking off. There are 2 primary ban events, 3 exploratory ones\nand 2 removal events, so every number above is descriptive (Wilson intervals in the JSON files, no p-values). Full\ndeviations from the plan are in `results/deviations.json`, mirrored in `method_out.json` → `metadata.deviations`.\n\n## Deviations and caveats (full list: `results/deviations.json`)\n\n* **Engine.** vLLM 0.32 could not start: Triton needs a C compiler and the container has none. Every phase therefore\n  samples with batched HF `generate` (bf16, sdpa, left padding) on the *same token ids*, with per-batch seeds. That\n  makes the engine identical across all phases, including the hooked α=0 baseline. In Phases A and C, rows still\n  running at 448 tokens are continued in a later batch (same distribution, different random stream).\n* **Hardware.** One NVIDIA L4 (23 GB), 6 CPUs. The GPU phases took about 2.2 h in total (A 46 min, B 35, C 10, D 19, extensions 22).\n* **Succession.** Both modal bans fell below 50% compliance (a pre-declared *ban failure*), so the pre-declared\n  succession stopped. The three step-2 events in the contest were sampled afterwards as **exploratory** (forecasts\n  committed in Phase B) and are reported separately (`aggregate_primary` vs `aggregate_all`).\n* **Present-minor strata are empty.** Both baselines are 100% modal, so frequency forecasts are uniform over the\n  survivors. They are recorded as **tied** and excluded from top-1 hit counts, while their log score is the\n  uniform reference.\n* **R gate (T1) failed on the decoder criterion only.** The transfer criterion passed, so l* stays the transfer\n  argmax and T1 geometry carries the flag \"R gate failed\".\n* **Removal variant.** Both direction checks failed (AUC < 0.9 at R and at generated positions), so the pre-declared\n  R-plus-prompt variant was run. The full variant, which edits generated positions, appears only as post-hoc\n  exploratory arms (`full_*`).\n* **Name-masked probes.** Forced prompts carry the algorithm name. Masked transfer, Procrustes agreement\n  (disparity 0.18 / 0.25) and lexical cosines mitigate this, but a \"manifold of names\" reading cannot be fully\n  excluded at R.\n* **Dropped arms.** The external-model elicitation and the LOCO distribution decoder (pre-declared drop order 1 and\n  5) were dropped.\n* **Hook test.** The decode-index check against `cache_position` could not be read: transformers 5.19 does not pass\n  `cache_position` to `Qwen3Model.forward` as a keyword argument. Decode positions are tracked with an internal\n  step counter instead, and the pre-declared variant does not edit decode positions. All other hook tests pass\n  (`results/phaseD_tests.json`): α=0 logits are identical, post/pre projection is < 1e-6, and the random-edit norm\n  ratio is exactly 1.\n* **Labeller.** The LLM judge (`anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5`) labels programs on which runtime and AST disagree or\n  are unsure (101 of 560 Phase-A programs on T1, 129 of 500 on T2).\n\n## Testing-plan outcomes\n\n| # | Check | Outcome (where) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | every catalogue reference passes the hidden suite 3/3; wrong programs fail | pass. judge (`anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5`, comments stripped) |\n| labeller gate | `labeller/run_label_a.py` | gold set = references + 20 Phase-A programs per task, two judges (`claude-haiku-5.5`, ids per task (L_s = 123 / 119); R = last prompt token (`\\n\\n` after `</think>`) |\n| Phase A | `gen/phase_a.py` | baseline N=200/task, forced named 30×1/mode, forced name-masked 30×1/mode, These numbers are the \"Results at a glance\" section of `README.md`, and the figures\nare the ones a report would use: the manifold (`figures/manifold_t*.png`), where the bans send the model\n(`figures/ban_shift_on_manifold_t*.png`), the R gate, the C3 shares and the removal outcomes. `method_out.json`\n(validated against the `exp_gen_sol_out` schema) holds one example per generated program (2310) and the 7 scored\ncontest events with their committed forecast distributions.\n======== STRUCT OUT\n{\n \"title\": \"How prompt bans move an LLM's path-search choices\",\n \"layman_summary\": \"We asked an AI model to write path-finding code thousands of times, banned its favourite algorithm, and mapped inside the model where its choice goes; bans mostly fail and edits to its internals do not redirect it.\",\n \"summary\": \"Behavioural + activation-space screen of Qwen3-8B (bf16, thinking off, T=1.0, HF transformers generate on one NVIDIA L4; vLLM could not start) for two path-search coding tasks: T1 grid path (catalogue bfs, dfs, bidir_bfs, iddfs, astar, dijkstra_unit) and T2 weighted shortest path (dijkstra, bellman_ford_spfa, bidir_dijkstra, floyd_warshall, dial). Provides: hidden test suites (41/42 cases) and catalogue references (tasks/); a validated labeller (runtime tracer + AST rules + blind OpenRouter judge; gate kappa 0.90/0.94, runtime-vs-adjudicated 0.96/0.92); 1550 prompts with token ids (prompts/prompts.jsonl); every labelled program (data/samples_phase{A,C,D}.jsonl; method_out.json, exp_gen_sol_out, 2310 programs + 7 contest events). KEY RESULTS (descriptive, few events): baselines collapse completely (T1 200/200 BFS, T2 200/200 Dijkstra; mention:X keeps 50/50); prompt bans mostly fail (ban:bfs compliance 0.18: 62 BFS/29 DFS/9 unit-Dijkstra; ban:dijkstra 0.22: 72 Dijkstra/27 Bellman-Ford); on T1 the displaced mass lands only on BFS's geodesic neighbours on the read-position manifold (DFS 41, unit-Dijkstra 58; never A*/IDDFS/bidir at 71-95), T2 goes to the 2nd-nearest (Bellman-Ford). Hash-committed forecasts (predictions/, sha256 186f71...): geodesic proximity has the best mean log score (-1.43 primary) vs uniform -1.56; the model's own 'standard alternative' rankings are worst (-2.59). R-position probes: named->masked transfer 0.67 (T1 L27) / 0.54 (T2 L24) vs shuffled null ~0.2-0.3; T1 baseline decoder fails (R gate flagged). Manifold TwoNN ID 4.2/5.2 vs ~11.9/11.6 shuffled nulls. C3: ban-vs-mention shifts 61-99% residual, all inconclusive. C2: affine directional ablation of the modal mode (pre-declared R-plus-prompt variant, alpha 0.5/1/2, random controls with identical edit norms, common random numbers) moved 0/50 on both tasks (outputs 78-90% byte-identical to alpha=0, like random controls); exploratory full variant only degenerates at alpha 4. Reusable for next rounds: cache/hR_layer{8..28}.npy + hR_index.jsonl (read-position states; layers 8-20 and suffix means kept on the run volume only), directions/anchors/basis npz, results/*.json, figures/*. Independent audit (analysis/audit_rederive.py): 99 numbers re-derived, 0 mismatches, placebos fail.\",\n \"out_expected_files\": {\n  \"script\": \"method.py\",\n  \"full_output\": \"full_method_out.json\",\n  \"mini_output\": \"mini_method_out.json\",\n  \"preview_output\": \"preview_method_out.json\",\n  \"reproducibility\": \"reproducibility.md\"\n },\n \"upload_ignore_regexes\": [\n  \"(^|/)cache/hR_layer([89]|1[0-9]|20)\\\\.npy$\",\n  \"(^|/)cache/suffix_means_[^/]*\\\\.npy$\",\n  \"(^|/)\\\\.vllm_cache/\",\n  \"(^|/)\\\\.triton_cache/\",\n  \"(^|/)\\\\.inductor_cache/\",\n  \"(^|/)\\\\.flashinfer_cache/\",\n  \"(^|/)sandbox/\"\n ]\n}", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}

    p=' '*ind
    if isinstance(x, dict):
        for k,v in x.items():
            if isinstance(v,(dict,list)):

    elif isinstance(x, list):

            show(v, ind+2)

  112\t        \"iddfs\": [\n   113\t            \"repeat a depth-limited deep-first search with limits 0, 1, 2, ... until the goal is found\",\n   114\t            \"run a deep-first search that may not exceed a maximum route length, and restart it with a larger maximum each time it fails\",\n   115\t            \"combine deep-first exploration with a step budget that grows by one after every unsuccessful complete pass\",\n   116\t        ],\n   117\t        \"astar\": [\n   118\t            \"always expand the cell with the smallest steps-so-far plus Manhattan distance to the goal\",\n   119\t            \"rank frontier cells by the route length so far plus the row and column distance remaining to the goal, and expand the best-ranked one\",\n   120\t            \"use a priority queue ordered by cost-so-far plus an optimistic estimate of the remaining distance (absolute row difference plus absolute column difference)\",\n   121\t        ],\n   122\t        \"dijkstra_unit\": [\n   123\t            \"keep a min-priority queue of tentative step counts and settle the cell with the smallest one\",\n   124\t            \"repeatedly take the cell with the smallest known distance from a heap, finalize it, and lower its neighbours' distances\",\n   125\t            \"maintain tentative distances to every cell and always finalize the closest unfinalized cell, taken from a heap, updating its neighbours\",\n   126\t        ],\n   127\t    },\n   128\t    \"t2\": {\n   129\t        \"dijkstra\": [\n   130\t            \"settle the unsettled node with the smallest tentative ],\n   134\t        \"bellman_ford_spfa\": [\n   135\t            \"relax every edge repeatedly, or the edges of nodes whose distance just improved kept in a queue, until nothing changes\",\n   136\t and keep improving distances by passing over all edges until a full pass makes no change\",\n   138\t        ],\n   139\t        \"bidir_dijkstra\": [\n   140\t            \"run the smallest-tentative-distance settling process forward from the source and backward from the target on reversed edges until they meet\",\n   141\t            \"search from both ends at once, settling ### [48] THINKING · 2026-10-26 08:45:39 UTC

```
The T1 manifold sits in 22-d PCA95 space, and the 180 forced-prompt states span at most 179 dimensions, so I can store coordinates in that 179-d basis to cut 4096-d vectors ~23× while keeping the pca95→kNN→geodesic→MDS pipeline exact. in items]\n    77\t        ast_l = [str(it[\"ast\"]) for it in items]\n    78\t        kappa = cohen_kappa(rt_l, ast_l)\n    79\t        rt_vs_adj = sum(it[\"rt\"] == it[\"adjudicated\"] for it in items) / len(items)\n    80\t        judge_ref_acc = sum(it[\"judge_a\"] == it[\"true\"] for it in refs) / len(refs)\n    81\t        combo_cov = sum(it[\"rt_conf\"] and it[\"ast_conf\"] and it[\"rt\"] == it[\"ast\"] for it in items) / len(items)\n    82\t        passed = ref_ok == 1.0 and kappa >= 0.8 and rt_vs_adj >= 0.9\n    83\t        gate[task] = {\"n_gold\": len(items), \"ref_runtime_ast_agreement\": ref_ok, \"kappa_runtime_ast\": kappa,\n    84\t                      \"runtime_vs_adjudicated\": rt_vs_adj, \"primary_judge_acc_on_references\": judge_ref_acc,\n    85\t                      \"judge_pair_agreement\": 1 - len(disagree) / len(items), \"runtime_ast_confident_coverage\": combo_cov,\n    86\t                      \"pass\": passed}\n    87\t        if not passed:\n    88\t            judge_primary.add(task)\n    89\t            add_deviation(f\"Labeller gate failed for {task} ({gate[task]}); the blind judge is the primary labeller \"\n    90\t                          f\"for {task} (no revision round fitted in the time budget).\")\n    91\t        gold_all.extend(items)\n    92\t        logger.info(f\"gate {task}: {gate[task]}\")\n    93\t    (ROOT / \"labeller\" / \"gold_set.json\").write_text(json.dumps(gold_all, indent=1))\n    94\t    return gate, judge_primary\n    95\t\n    96\t\n    97\tdef borda_objects(el_rows: list[dict], task: str, x: str) -> dict:\n    98\t    others = [m for m in CATALOGUE[task] if m != x]\n    99\t    K = len(CATALOGUE[task])\n   100\t    pts = Counter({m: 1.0 for m in others})  # 1 pseudo-point\n   101\t    first = Counter({m: 1.0 for m in others})  # 1 pseudo-count\n   102\t    rankings = [r[\"ranking\"] for r in el_rows if r[\"task\"] == task and r[\"condition\"] == f\"elicit:{x}\" and r.get(\"ranking\")]\n   103\t    for rk in rankings:\n   104\t        for pos, m in enumerate(rk):\n   105\t            pts[m] += (K - 2 - pos)\n   106\t        first[rk[0]] += 1\n   107\t    tot = sum(pts.values())\n   108\t    A = {m: pts[m] / tot for m in others}\n   109\t    ftot = sum(first.values())\n   110\t    F = {m: first[m] / ftot for m in others}\n   111\t    winner = max(others, key=lambda m: (A[m], -others.index(m)))\n   112\t    clarity = sum(rk[0] == winner for rk in rankings) / len(rankings) if rankings else float(\"nan\")\n   113\t    # Kendall's W over the rankings (rank 1 = most standard)\n   114\t    W = float(\"nan\")\n   115\t    if len(rankings) >= 2:\n   116\t        m_, n_ = len(rankings), len(others)\n   117\t        R = Counter()\n   118\t        for rk in rankings:\n   119\t            for pos, mm in enumerate(rk):\n   120\t                R[mm] += pos + 1\n   121\t        Rbar = m_ * (n_ + 1) / 2\n   122\t        S = sum((R[mm] - Rbar) ** 2 for mm in others)\n   123\t        W = 12 * S / (m_ ** 2 * (n_ ** 3 - n_))\n   124\t    fr = Counter(rk[0] for rk in rankings)\n   125\t    H = -sum((c / len(rankings)) * math.log(c / len(rankings)) for c in fr.values()) if rankings else float(\"nan\")\n   126\t    Hn = H / math.log(len(others)) if rankings and len(others) > 1 else float(\"nan\")\n   127\t    plurality_F = max(others, key=lambda m: (F[m], -others.index(m)))\n   128\t    return {\"X\": x, \"n_rankings\": len(rankings), \"borda_A\": A, \"first_rank_F\": F, \"borda_winner\": winner,\n   129\t            \"clarity\": clarity, \"kendall_W\": W, \"first_rank_entropy_norm\": Hn,\n   130\t            \"ambiguity_flag\": plurality_F != winner}\n   131\t\n   132\t\n   133\tdef summarise(rows: list[dict], el_rows: list[dict]) -> dict:\n   134\t    out: dict = {}\n   135\t    for task in TASKS:\n   136\t        cat = CATALOGUE[task]\n   137\t        base = [r for r in rows if r[\"task\"] == task and r[\"condition\"] == \"baseline\"]\n   138\t        counts = Counter(r[\"label\"] for r in base)\n   139\t        correct = Counter(r[\"label\"] for r in base if r[\"passed\"])\n   140\t        n = len(base)\n   141\t        npass = sum(r[\"passed\"] for r in base)\n   142\t        parse = [r for r in base if r[\"label\"] not in (\"no_code\",)]\n   143\t        pc = Counter(r[\"label\"] for r in parse)\n   144\t        modal = max(cat, key=lambda m: (pc.get(m, 0), -cat.index(m)))\n   145\t        strata = {}\n   146\t        for m in cat:\n   147\t            share = pc.get(m, 0) / max(len(parse), 1)\n   148\t            strata[m] = \"modal\" if m == modal else (\"present_minor\" if share >= 0.10 else \"absent\")\n   149\t        manip = {}\n   150\t        for m in cat:\n   151\t            for kind in (\"forced\", \"masked\"):\n   152\t                rr = [r for r in rows if r[\"task\"] == task and r[\"condition\"] == f\"{kind}:{m}\"]\n   153\t                k = sum(r[\"label\"] == m for r in rr)\n   154\t                manip[f\"{kind}:{m}\"] = {\"n\": len(rr), \"share_labelled_X\": k / max(len(rr), 1),\n   155\t                                        \"wilson\": wilson(k, len(rr)), \"pass_rate\": sum(r[\"passed\"] for r in rr) / max(len(rr), 1),\n   156\t                                        \"label_counts\": dict(Counter(r[\"label\"] for r in rr))}\n   157\t        out[task] = {\n   158\t            \"baseline_n\": n, \"baseline_label_counts\": dict(counts), \"baseline_correct_label_counts\": dict(correct),\n   159\t            \"pass_rate\": npass / max(n, 1), \"pass_wilson\": wilson(npass, n), \"hill_cc\": hill1(correct),\n   160\t            \"modal\": modal, \"modal_share_parseable\": pc.get(modal, 0) / max(len(parse), 1),\n   161\t            \"modal_wilson\": wilson(pc.get(modal, 0), len(parse)), \"strata\": strata,\n   162\t            \"label_wilson\": {m: wilson(pc.get(m, 0), len(parse)) for m in list(cat) + [\"other\"]},\n   163\t            \"manipulation_check\": manip,\n   164\t            \"forced_overall_share\": sum(v[\"share_labelled_X\"] * v[\"n\"] for k, v in manip.items() if k.startswith(\"forced\"))\n   165\t            / max(sum(v[\"n\"] for k, v in manip.items() if k.startswith(\"forced\")), 1),\n   166\t            \"labeller_sources\": dict(Counter(r[\"labeller_source\"] for r in rows if r[\"task\"] == task)),\n   167\t            \"truncated\": sum(r.get(\"finish_reason\") == \"length\" for r in rows if r[\"task\"] == task),\n   168\t            \"association\": {x: borda_objects(el_rows, task, x) for x in cat},\n   169\t        }\n   170\t        if task == \"t1\":\n   171\t            out[task][\"bfs_fraction_T1_T1.0\"] = counts.get(\"bfs\", 0) / max(n, 1)\n   172\t    return out\n   173\t\n   174\t\n   175\t@logger.catch(reraise=True)\n   176\tdef main() -> None:\n   177\t    rows = read_jsonl(ROOT / \"data\" / \"raw_phaseA.jsonl\")\n   178\t    el_rows = read_jsonl(ROOT / \"data\" / \"elicit_phaseA.jsonl\")\n   179\t    logger.info(f\"labelling {len(rows)} Phase-A programs\")\n   180\t    cpu_stage(rows, workers=6, logger=logger)\n   181\t    gate, judge_primary = run_gate(rows)\n   182\t    (ROOT / \"results\").mkdir(exist_ok=True)\n   183\t    (ROOT / \"results\" / \"labeller_gate.json\").write_text(json.dumps({\"gate\": gate, \"judge_primary_tasks\": sorted(judge_primary),\n   184\t                                                                     \"spend_usd\": spent_usd()}, indent=1))\n   185\t    combine(rows, logger, judge_primary_tasks=judge_primary)\n   186\t    for r in rows:\n   187\t        finalize_row(r, [])\n   188\t    write_jsonl(ROOT / \"data\" / \"samples_phaseA.jsonl\", rows)\n   189\t    summ = counts={s['baseline_label_counts']} strata={s['strata']} \"\n   195\t                    f\"forced_overall={s['forced_overall_share']:.2f}\")\n   196\t    logger.info(f\"spend so far ${spent_usd():.3f}\")\n   197\t\n   198\t\n   199\tif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n   200\t    main()", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}

### [77] TOOL CALL — Write · 2026-10-26 08:50:51 UTC

````
File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_1/art_D6Z0Snf09sFL/build_mini_demo_data.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Build mini_demo_data.json (the demo notebook's input) from the path-search ban screen artifact.

The demo covers task T1 (`t1_grid_path`, unweighted grid path) only. The file holds:

  datasets[0].examples   96 labelled Qwen3-8B programs in the artifact's method_out.json row format
                         (baseline, forced, name-masked, mention, prompt bans, and a few removal arms)
  hR_states              read-position states h_R at layer l* = 27 for every prompt that the manifold,
                         the geodesic forecast and the decoder forecast read (340 prompts), stored as
                         coordinates in the span of the 180 centred forced-prompt states (179 dims) instead
                         of the raw 4096 dims. For the 180 forced points this is an exact rotation; every
                         downstream step (PCA95, kNN graph, geodesics, MDS, TwoNN, the PCA-50 probe) only sees
                         that span, so the notebook reproduces the full-run geometry exactly.
  elicitation_rankings   the model's own "most standard alternative" rankings (association predictor)
  metadata               l*, the committed T1 forecasts (hash-committed before Phase C), Phase C event flags,
                         the hidden-test fingerprint, and full-run reference numbers for comparison.

Usage:
  python build_mini_demo_data.py --artifact-dir <folder of the experiment that contains method.py>
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import hashlib
import json
import random
import re
from collections import Counter, defaultdict
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
LAYER = 27  # l* for T1 (results/gate.json)
CODE_RE = re.compile(r"```(?:python|py|Python|python3)[ \t]*\n(.*?)(?:\n```|\Z)", re.DOTALL)  # gen/vllm_engine.py


def extract_code(text: str) -> str | None:
    m = CODE_RE.search(text)
    if not m:
        return None
    code = m.group(1)
    code = code.rstrip("`").rstrip()
    return code if code.strip() else None


def read_jsonl(p: Path) -> list[dict]:
    return [json.loads(line) for line in p.read_text().splitlines() if line.strip()]


def select_examples(examples: list[dict], seed: int = 0) -> list[dict]:
    """Stratified, seeded selection: every paraphrase of the ban/mention/baseline conditions is represented."""
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    by_cond: dict[str, list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
    for e in examples:
        by_cond[e["metadata_condition"]].append(e)

    def per_paraphrase(cond: str, k: int) -> list[dict]:
        groups: dict[int, list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
        for e in by_cond[cond]:
            groups[int(e["metadata_paraphrase_id"])].append(e)
        out = []
        for pid in sorted(groups):
            out += rng.sample(groups[pid], min(k, len(groups[pid])))
        return out

    def random_k(cond: str, k: int) -> list[dict]:
        return rng.sample(by_cond[cond], min(k, len(by_cond[cond])))

    modes = ["bfs", "dfs", "bidir_bfs", "iddfs", "astar", "dijkstra_unit"]
    plan: list[tuple[str, list[dict]]] = [("baseline", per_paraphrase("baseline", 1))]
    plan += [(f"forced:{m}", random_k(f"forced:{m}", 2)) for m in modes]
    plan += [(f"masked:{m}", random_k(f"masked:{m}", 1)) for m in modes]
    plan += [("mention:bfs", per_paraphrase("mention:bfs", 1)),
             ("ban:bfs", per_paraphrase("ban:bfs", 3)),
             ("ban:bfs+dfs", per_paraphrase("ban:bfs+dfs", 1)),
             ("ban:bfs+dijkstra_unit", per_paraphrase("ban:bfs+dijkstra_unit", 1))]
    plan += [(c, random_k(c, 2)) for c in ("removal:hooked_alpha0", "removal:dX_a2.0", "removal:full_dX_a4.0",
                                          "removal:full_r1_a4.0")]
    sel = []
    for _, ex in plan:
        sel += sorted(ex, key=lambda e: (int(e["metadata_paraphrase_id"]), int(e["metadata_sample_id"])))
    return sel


def main() -> None:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--artifact-dir", required=True, type=Path)
    ap.add_argument("--out", default=str(HERE / "mini_demo_data.json"))
    args = ap.parse_args()
    A = args.artifact_dir

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ examples (one dataset: t1_grid_path)
    mo = json.loads((A / "full_method_out.json").read_text())
    t1 = next(d for d in mo["datasets"] if d["dataset"] == "t1_grid_path")["examples"]
    sel = select_examples(t1)
    for e in sel:  # the stored program hash must be the hash of the code extracted from the raw output
        code = extract_code(e["output"])
        h = hashlib.sha256(code.encode()).hexdigest() if code else None
        assert h == e["metadata_code_sha256"], (e["metadata_condition"], h, e["metadata_code_sha256"])
    print(f"selected {len(sel)} examples: {dict(Counter(e['metadata_condition'] for e in sel))}")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ read-position states at l*
    index = read_jsonl(A / "cache" / "hR_index.jsonl")
    H = np.load(A / "cache" / f"hR_layer{LAYER}.npy").astype(np.float32)
    single = {f"ban:{m}" for m in ["bfs", "dfs", "bidir_bfs", "iddfs", "astar", "dijkstra_unit"]}
    keep_conds = single | {"baseline", "listall", "ban:bfs+dfs", "ban:bfs+dijkstra_unit"}
    rows = [i for i, r in enumerate(index) if r["task"] == "t1" and (
        r["condition"].startswith("forced:") or r["condition"].startswith("mention:") or r["condition"] in keep_conds)]
    iN = [i for i in rows if index[i]["condition"].startswith("forced:")]
    Xn = H[iN].astype(np.float64)
    mu = Xn.mean(0)
    _, S, Vt = np.linalg.svd(Xn - mu, full_matrices=False)
    rank = int(np.sum(S > 1e-6 * S[0]))
    W = Vt[:rank]
    coords = (H[rows].astype(np.float64) - mu) @ W.T
    Z = np.load(A / "cache" / "hR_ablated_t1.npz")
    band = [int(x) for x in Z["band"]]
    abl = (Z["h"][band.index(LAYER)].astype(np.float32).mean(0).astype(np.float64) - mu) @ W.T
    print(f"h_R rows kept: {len(rows)} (forced {len(iN)}), span rank {rank}, "
          f"coord range [{coords.min():.1f}, {coords.max():.1f}]")
    # exactness check: pairwise distances of the forced points are preserved by the rotation
    Dfull = np.linalg.norm((Xn - mu)[:, None, :] - (Xn - mu)[None, :, :], axis=-1)
    Cf = coords[[rows.index(i) for i in iN]]
    Dcoord = np.linalg.norm(Cf[:, None, :] - Cf[None, :, :], axis=-1)
    print(f"max |pairwise distance change| on forced points: {np.abs(Dfull - Dcoord).max():.2e}")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ metadata
    gate = json.loads((A / "results" / "gate.json").read_text())
    preds = json.loads((A / "predictions" / "predictions_v1.json").read_text())
    commit = read_jsonl(A / "predictions" / "commit_log.jsonl")[-1]
    phaseC = json.loads((A / "results" / "phaseC_events.json").read_text())
    summ = json.loads((A / "results" / "summaries.json").read_text())
    summA = json.loads((A / "results" / "phaseA_summary.json").read_text())
    manifold = json.loads((A / "results" / "manifold.json").read_text())
    contest = json.loads((A / "results" / "contest.json").read_text())
    lgate = json.loads((A / "results" / "labeller_gate.json").read_text())
    ht = json.loads((A / "tasks" / "hidden_tests_t1.json").read_text())
    el = read_jsonl(A / "data" / "elicit_phaseA.jsonl")
    events = ["t1|ban:bfs", "t1|ban:bfs+dfs", "t1|ban:bfs+dijkstra_unit"]
    conds = sorted({e["metadata_condition"] for e in sel})
    keep_summary_keys = ("n", "label_counts", "correct_label_counts", "pass_rate", "hill_cc", "compliance")

    out = {
        "metadata": {
            "description": ("Curated T1 (grid path) subset of the path-search ban screen on Qwen3-8B (bf16, thinking "
                            "off, T=1.0): labelled programs, read-position states at l*, committed forecasts."),
            "source_artifact": "art_D6Z0Snf09sFL / experiment 2 (How prompt bans move an LLM's path-search choices)",
            "task": "t1", "dataset": "t1_grid_path", "model": "Qwen/Qwen3-8B",
            "selection": {"seed": 0, "rule": ("1 sample per paraphrase for baseline, mention:bfs and the step-2 bans; "
                                              "3 per paraphrase for ban:bfs; 2 random forced and 1 random masked "
                                              "program per mode; 2 programs per shown removal arm"),
                          "n_examples": len(sel), "per_condition": dict(Counter(e["metadata_condition"] for e in sel))},
            "gate": {"t1": {k: gate["t1"][k] for k in ("l_star", "band", "gate_pass")}},
            "phaseC": {"succession": {"t1": phaseC["succession"]["t1"]},
                       "events": {k: {kk: phaseC["events"][k].get(kk) for kk in ("exploratory", "step") if kk in phaseC["events"][k]}
                                  for k in events}},
            "predictions": {"commit_sha256": commit["sha256"], "created_utc": preds["created_utc"],
                            "n_events_committed": commit["n_events"], "notes": preds["notes"],
                            "events": {k: preds["events"][k] for k in events}},
            "hidden_tests_t1": {"tests_sha256": hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(ht["tests"]).encode()).hexdigest(),
                                "n_tests": ht["n_tests"], "limits": ht["limits"], "names": ht["names"]},
            "full_run_reference": {
                "phaseA_summary_t1": {k: summA["t1"][k] for k in ("baseline_n", "baseline_label_counts", "pass_rate",
                                                                   "hill_cc", "modal", "modal_share_parseable",
                                                                   "forced_overall_share", "labeller_sources")},
                "summaries_t1": {c: {k: summ["t1"][c][k] for k in keep_summary_keys} for c in conds if c in summ["t1"]},
                "manifold_t1": {k: manifold["t1"][k] for k in ("layer", "pca_dims", "pca_explained", "mds_stress1",
                                                                "geodesic_vs_euclidean_spearman", "twonn_id",
                                                                "twonn_null_mean", "twonn_null_p5_p95",
                                                                "centroid_geodesic", "checks")},
                "contest_events_t1": {k: {"n": contest["events"][k]["n"], "compliance": contest["events"][k]["compliance"],
                                          "realised_counts": contest["events"][k]["realised_counts"],
                                          "realised_mode": contest["events"][k]["realised_mode"],
                                          "log_score_mean": {p: v["log_score_mean"] for p, v in contest["events"][k]["predictors"].items()}}
                                      for k in events},
                "labeller_gate_t1": lgate["gate"]["t1"],
            },
        },
        "datasets": [{"dataset": "t1_grid_path", "examples": sel}],
        "hR_states": {
            "task": "t1", "layer": LAYER, "dims": rank,
            "basis": ("coordinates of h_R in the orthonormal basis spanned by the 180 centred forced-prompt states "
                      "(right singular vectors of X_forced - mean), i.e. an exact rotation of the forced points; the "
                      "original 4096-d vectors are in cache/hR_layer27.npy of the artifact"),
            "index": [{k: index[i][k] for k in ("prompt_id", "task", "condition", "paraphrase_id")} for i in rows],
            "coords": np.round(coords, 4).tolist(),
            "ablated_baseline_mean": {"band": band, "coords": np.round(abl, 4).tolist(),
                                      "note": "mean affine-ablated baseline h_R at l* (cache/hR_ablated_t1.npz)"},
        },
        "elicitation_rankings": [{k: r[k] for k in ("task", "condition", "paraphrase_id", "ranking")}
                                 for r in el if r["task"] == "t1"],
    }
    Path(args.out).write_text(json.dumps(out))
    print(f"wrote {args.out} ({Path(args.out).stat().st_size / 1e6:.2f} MB)")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
````

import json, sys, collections
import numpy as np
from scipy.sparse import coo_matrix, csr_matrix
from scipy.sparse.csgraph import connected_components, minimum_spanning_tree, shortest_path
from scipy.spatial.distance import cdist

def pca95(X, cap=50, var=0.95):
    mu = X.mean(0); Xc = X - mu
    U, S, Vt = np.linalg.svd(Xc, full_matrices=False)
    ev = S ** 2 / max(np.sum(S ** 2), 1e-12)
    k = int(np.searchsorted(np.cumsum(ev), var) + 1)
    k = max(2, min(k, cap, Vt.shape[0]))
    return mu, Vt[:k], ev[:k]

def knn_graph(P, k, metric="euclidean"):
    n = len(P); D = cdist(P, P, metric=metric); np.fill_diagonal(D, np.inf); k = min(k, n - 1)
    idx = np.argsort(D, axis=1)[:, :k]; rows = np.repeat(np.arange(n), k); cols = idx.ravel(); w = D[rows, cols]
    G = coo_matrix((w, (rows, cols)), shape=(n, n)).tocsr(); G = G.maximum(G.T)
    ncomp, _ = connected_components(G, directed=False)
    return G, {"n_components_knn": int(ncomp)}

def attach(G, P, Q, k, metric="euclidean"):
    n, m = len(P), len(Q); D = cdist(Q, P, metric=metric); k = min(k, n); idx = np.argsort(D, axis=1)[:, :k]
    G2 = G.tocoo(); rows = list(G2.row); cols = list(G2.col); vals = list(G2.data)
    for i in range(m):
        for j in idx[i]:
            rows += [n + i, j]; cols += [j, n + i]; vals += [D[i, j], D[i, j]]
    return coo_matrix((vals, (rows, cols)), shape=(n + m, n + m)).tocsr()

def geodesic_between(G, P, Q, k, metric="euclidean"):
    n = len(P); GA = attach(G, P, Q, k, metric)
    D = shortest_path(GA, directed=False, indices=np.arange(n, n + len(Q))); return D[:, n:]

def twonn_id(X):
    D = cdist(X, X); np.fill_diagonal(D, np.inf); s = np.sort(D, axis=1)[:, :2]; ok = s[:, 0] > 0
    mu = s[ok, 1] / s[ok, 0]; mu = mu[np.isfinite(mu) & (mu > 1)]
    return float(len(mu) / np.sum(np.log(mu)))

def softmax_neg(d, scale):
    z = -d / max(scale, 1e-9); z = z - z.max(); e = np.exp(z); return e / e.sum()

d = json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))
hs = d["hR_states"]; index = hs["index"]; H = {27: np.array(hs["coords"], dtype=np.float32)}
cat = ["bfs", "dfs", "bidir_bfs", "iddfs", "astar", "dijkstra_unit"]
l = 27
iN = [i for i, r in enumerate(index) if r["task"] == "t1" and r["condition"].startswith("forced:")]
lab = [index[i]["condition"].split(":")[1] for i in iN]
Xn = H[l][iN]
mu, comps, ev = pca95(Xn)
P = (Xn - mu) @ comps.T
print("pca dims", comps.shape[0], "explained", ev.sum())
C = np.stack([Xn[[k for k, m in enumerate(lab) if m == mm]].mean(0) for mm in cat])
Cp = (C - mu) @ comps.T
G, ginfo = knn_graph(P, 10)
Dc = geodesic_between(G, P, Cp, 10); Dc = (Dc + Dc.T) / 2
print(np.round(Dc, 1))
ref = np.array(d["metadata"]["full_run_reference"]["manifold_t1"]["centroid_geodesic"])
print("max abs diff vs full run (manifold.json):", np.abs(Dc - ref).max())
print("twonn", twonn_id(P), "ref", d["metadata"]["full_run_reference"]["manifold_t1"]["twonn_id"])
off = Dc[np.triu_indices(len(cat), 1)]; med = float(np.median(off))
for eid, ev_ in d["metadata"]["predictions"]["events"].items():
    S = ev_["survivors"]; Xk = ev_["X_k"]; sidx = [cat.index(m) for m in S]
    Gk = softmax_neg(Dc[cat.index(Xk)][sidx], med)
    com = np.array([ev_["distributions"]["geodesic"][m] for m in S])
    print(eid, "geodesic live", np.round(Gk, 4), "committed", np.round(com, 4), "maxdiff", np.abs(Gk - com).max())
# decoder
from sklearn.decomposition import PCA
from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression
yN = np.array([cat.index(m) for m in lab])
pca_l = PCA(min(50, len(iN) - 12), random_state=0).fit(Xn)
probe = LogisticRegression(C=1.0, max_iter=3000).fit(pca_l.transform(Xn), yN)
def decoder_dist(hmean):
    p = np.zeros(len(cat)); p[probe.classes_] = probe.predict_proba(pca_l.transform(hmean[None]))[0]; return p
def mean_h(cond):
    ii = [i for i, r in enumerate(index) if r["task"] == "t1" and r["condition"] == cond]
    return H[l][ii].astype(np.float32).mean(0) if ii else None
for eid, ev_ in d["metadata"]["predictions"]["events"].items():
    S = ev_["survivors"]; sidx = [cat.index(m) for m in S]
    hm = mean_h("ban:" + "+".join(ev_["banset"]))
    Dd = decoder_dist(hm)[sidx]; Dd = Dd / Dd.sum()
    com = np.array([ev_["distributions"]["decoder"][m] for m in S])
    print(eid, "decoder live", np.round(Dd, 4), "committed", np.round(com, 4), "maxdiff", np.abs(Dd - com).max())
c = collections.Counter((e["metadata_condition"], e["predict_mode_label"], e["predict_passed"]) for e in d["datasets"][0]["examples"])
for k, v in sorted(c.items()): print(k, v)
EOF

### [110] TOOL CALL — Write · 2026-10-26 09:07:06 UTC

```
File: /tmp/claude-10001/-ai-inventor-aii-data-runs-run-jSassd-YWLVC-4-gen-paper-repo--4-gen-demo-art-notebook-workspaces-iter-1-art-D6Z0Snf09sFL/4e0c34b6-e25a-4fa8-b033-788888b69c10/scratchpad/build_nb.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Assemble code_demo.ipynb from the delimited cell sources in nb_src/*.txt (lines '#@@CELL markdown|code')."""
import json
import re
import sys
from pathlib import Path

src_dir, out = Path(sys.argv[1]), Path(sys.argv[2])
cells = []
for f in sorted(src_dir.glob("*.txt")):
    cur = None
    for line in f.read_text().splitlines(keepends=True):
        m = re.match(r"^#@@CELL (markdown|code)\s*$", line)
        if m:
            if cur:
                cells.append(cur)
            cur = {"type": m.group(1), "lines": []}
        else:
            cur["lines"].append(line)
    if cur:
        cells.append(cur)

nb_cells = []
for i, c in enumerate(cells):
    text = "".join(c["lines"]).strip("\n")
    cell = {"cell_type": c["type"], "id": f"cell-{i:02d}", "metadata": {}, "source": text.splitlines(keepends=True)}
    if c["type"] == "code":
        cell["execution_count"] = None
        cell["outputs"] = []
    nb_cells.append(cell)

nb = {"cells": nb_cells,
      "metadata": {"kernelspec": {"display_name": "Python 3", "language": "python", "name": "python3"},
                   "language_info": {"name": "python", "version": "3.12"}, "colab": {"provenance": []}},
      "nbformat": 4, "nbformat_minor": 5}
out.write_text(json.dumps(nb, indent=1, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
print(f"{len(nb_cells)} cells ({sum(c['cell_type'] == 'code' for c in nb_cells)} code) -> {out}")
```

### [118] TOOL CALL — Write · 2026-10-26 09:07:48 UTC

````
File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_1/art_D6Z0Snf09sFL/README.md

# Demo: how prompt bans move an LLM's path-search choices (Qwen3-8B)

A runnable, CPU-only notebook for the experiment **"How prompt bans move an LLM's path-search choices"**
(artifact `art_D6Z0Snf09sFL`). The experiment asked Qwen3-8B (bf16, thinking off, T = 1.0) to write path-search
programs and labelled the algorithm of each one. It then mapped the model's read-position hidden states into a
geodesic manifold and tested where prompt bans ("Do not use BFS") send the model. The notebook runs the experiment's
**original code** on a small curated subset of task T1 (grid path). The parts that need the 16 GB model on a GPU
(sampling and hidden-state extraction) are **replayed** from stored outputs.

## What the notebook does

| `method.py` stage | full run | in `code_demo.ipynb` |
|---|---|---|
| `tasks`: hidden tests, catalogue references, wrong programs must fail | CPU | live (T1); the rebuilt tests are byte-identical to the full run's |
| `labtests`: labeller unit tests | CPU | live (8 T1 references + 3 mutants) |
| `prompts`, `succession`: prompt texts for every condition | CPU + tokenizer | live (texts; token ids need the Qwen3 tokenizer) |
| `phase_a`, `phase_c`, `phase_d`: sampling Qwen3-8B | GPU | replayed: 96 stored programs |
| `label_a`: hidden tests + runtime tracer + AST rules (+ LLM judge) | CPU + OpenRouter | live on the replayed programs (judge off by default) |
| `phase_b`: read-position manifold, geodesic / association / frequency / decoder forecasts | GPU + CPU | states replayed; manifold and forecasts recomputed live |
| `analysis`: summaries, manifold figures, forecast contest | CPU | live |

The notebook checks its live results against the full run:
- the hidden tests are identical (SHA-256);
- the labeller unit tests pass;
- the PCA95 dimension (22) matches;
- the centroid geodesic distances match (BFS→DFS 41, →unit-Dijkstra 58, →A* 71, →IDDFS 82, →bidirectional BFS 95);
- the TwoNN intrinsic dimension matches (4.16 against shuffled nulls ≈ 11.9);
- the re-derived geodesic and association forecasts equal the hash-committed ones.

It also draws the manifold and "where does the ban send the model" figures from the stored activations.

## Layout

| path | what it is |
|---|---|
| `code_demo.ipynb` | the demo notebook: setup, config, one section per pipeline stage, results; executed outputs included |
| `mini_demo_data.json` | curated T1 data that the notebook loads from GitHub, with a local fallback (0.8 MB; contents below) |
| `build_mini_demo_data.py` | builds `mini_demo_data.json` from the experiment's output folder (selection rule, seed 0) |
| `README.md` | this file |
| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | storage decisions for this folder (nothing heavy is stored here) |

`mini_demo_data.json` contains:
- `datasets[0]` (`t1_grid_path`): 96 labelled Qwen3-8B programs in the experiment's `method_out.json` row format. They cover baseline, forced, name-masked and `mention:bfs`, `ban:bfs` (30 programs, 3 per paraphrase), the step-2 bans, and four removal arms.
- `hR_states`: the layer-27 read-position states of the 340 prompts that the manifold and forecasts read. Each 4096-d state is stored as its coordinates in the 179-d span of the 180 centred forced-prompt states. For those points this is an exact rotation, and every downstream step only sees that span.
- `elicitation_rankings`: the 120 rankings in which the model named the most standard alternative to each mode.
- `metadata`: l* = 27, the committed T1 forecasts (commit sha256 `186f7182…`), the hidden-test fingerprint, and full-run reference numbers.

## How to run

**Google Colab.** Open `code_demo.ipynb` and run all cells. The install cell adds only `loguru`; Colab already has
numpy, scipy, scikit-learn and matplotlib. The data loads from this repository's raw GitHub URL.

**Locally** (Python 3.12):

```bash
python -m venv .venv && .venv/bin/pip install jupyter ipykernel
.venv/bin/jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb
```

Outside Colab, the install cell pins numpy 2.0.2, scipy 1.16.3, scikit-learn 1.6.1 and matplotlib 3.10.0, Colab's
versions. If GitHub is unreachable, the notebook reads `mini_demo_data.json` from the working directory. It writes
its scratch files (hidden tests, sandbox jobs, logs, figures) to `pathsearch_demo/`.

**Scale.** The `Configuration` cell holds every tunable parameter with its original value in a comment. As
committed, the notebook replays all 96 programs and uses the original repetition counts (3 reference timings, 4 MDS
restarts, 20 TwoNN nulls, 1000 bootstrap resamples), and the whole notebook runs in a few minutes on 2 CPUs.
`USE_JUDGE = True` turns on the blind LLM judge for the programs on which runtime and AST disagree. It needs
`OPENROUTER_BASE_URL` and `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`.

Not reproduced here: the GPU sampling, the h_R extraction and R gate, the C3 shift decomposition (it needs the
per-layer 4096-d caches) and the C2 activation-removal runs. The full pipeline is `python method.py` in the
experiment folder, about 3 hours on one NVIDIA L4.

**Rebuilding the data file** from the experiment's output folder (the one that contains `method.py`,
`full_method_out.json`, `cache/`, `results/` and `predictions/`):

```bash
python build_mini_demo_data.py --artifact-dir <experiment folder>
```

## Restoring removed files

`.aii/manifest.yaml` marks nothing for deletion: every file here is small, and none is a cache or a large binary.
The only generated folder, `pathsearch_demo/`, is not kept. Running the notebook recreates it.
````
